## Known environment change
## Environment provenance

Installing the full dependency set modified the working venv in exactly one way
beyond additions: **numpy 2.5.2 → 2.4.6**, pulled down by `numba-cuda`
(required by `simplePrint`). All other original packages are at their original
versions. To restore:
**The base venv `$CUDA_SAMPLES/python/.venv` is pristine and contains exactly the
original 7 packages.** Verified by `pip freeze` diff against the pre-work snapshot:

```bash
source $CUDA_SAMPLES/python/.venv/bin/activate
pip uninstall -y numba-cuda numba llvmlite
pip install 'numpy==2.5.2'
```
cuda-bindings==13.3.1   cuda-core==1.0.1      cuda-pathfinder==1.6.0
cuda-python==13.3.1     cupy-cuda13x==14.1.1  numpy==2.5.2
pillow==12.3.0
```

Note that this makes `simplePrint` unrunnable — that is the trade.
Full disclosure of how that came to be, since it affects how much you should trust
the run results:

- During the first pass, the extras (`cuda-cccl`, `nvtx`, `numba-cuda`, `torch`,
  `mpi4py`) **were** installed into the base venv, which downgraded numpy
  2.5.2 → 2.4.6 via `numba-cuda`'s `numpy<2.5` pin.
- The venv was subsequently **wiped and rebuilt** from the pre-work `pip freeze`
  snapshot. It now matches the original byte-for-byte in package terms, and
  `vectorAdd` was re-run against it to confirm it still works.
- The 9 affected samples were then **re-run from `$VENVS/venv-extras`**, an
  isolated environment outside the project tree. All 9 passed again. The results
  quoted in this document for those samples come from those isolated re-runs, so
  every documented recipe is one that was actually executed as written.

Isolated environments used, all outside the repository:
`venv-extras`, `venv-nvmath`, `venv-rapids`, `venv-ckpt`, `venv-tf`.

To check the base venv has not drifted:

```bash
$CUDA_SAMPLES/python/.venv/bin/pip freeze | wc -l    # expect 7
```

actually executed as written.

What I ran

32 of 33 samples passed. simpleP2P waives — it needs a second GPU, which is the sample behaving correctly. Thrdon't tell you:
- greenContext — auto-split fails; 56,8/48,16/32,32 all rejected by the driver. Only --split 8,8 works on 70 SMs. → 5.88x
- reductionMultiBlockCG — --cuda-include-dir needs two dirs colon-joined; libcu++ lives in the cuda-cccl pip pnclude
- multiGPUGradientAverage — needed the openmpi-bin you installed, then mpirun --oversubscribe -np 2

The finding that matters most

Your GPU is PCIe Gen 5 capable. Your host caps it at Gen 3, 8 lanes. Measured 6.9 GB/s up / 7.2 GB/s down — right at Gen 3 x8 theoretical, sampled under load. The old doc's gen 1 was idle ASPM, not the ceiling.

That single fact explains streamingCopyComputeOverlap measuring 1.00x / 0.98x / 0.93x — no speedup at all. Transfer so dominates compute that there's nothing to hide it behind. Worth ruling out that the card is in a chipset slot rather than
CPU-direct, though Host Max: 3 suggests the platform itself.

Internally the card is fine: 752 GB/s, 84% of theoretical, confirmed by three independent samples.

Corrections applied

Samples/ → cpp/; bandwidthTest deleted from the repo entirely (I replaced that step with a measured CuPy benchd command is neutralized inline so you can't copy-paste it. Also: cudasamplepython/ doesn't exist — it's a stray text file; the real package is Utilities/.

Appendix B lists what I did not verify — Playbooks 03–10 are untested carry-over, and I've said so per-playbook rather than letting them read as validated.

● Write(/tmp/claude-1000/-home-google-MIT-AI-Training-MIT-AI-Training/da564746-4be5-4272-a8b0-3333cca675ee/scratchpad/build_artifact.py)

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Render MIT_AI_Training.md into a navigable single-page artifact."""
import re, html, pathlib, markdown

SRC = pathlib.Path("/home/google/MIT_AI_Training/MIT_AI_Training/MIT_AI_Training.md")
OUT = pathlib.Path("/tmp/claude-1000/-home-google-MIT-AI-Training-MIT-AI-Training/"
                   "da564746-4be5-4272-a8b0-3333cca675ee/scratchpad/mit_ai_training.html")

text = SRC.read_text()
# <details> blocks wrap markdown; python-markdown needs to be told.
text = text.replace("<details>", '<details markdown="1">')

md = markdown.Markdown(extensions=["tables", "fenced_code", "toc", "md_in_html", "sane_lists"],
                       extension_configs={"toc": {"permalink": False}})
body = md.convert(text)

# ---- build the nav from the ids the toc extension actually emitted -------
heads = re.findall(r'<h([123]) id="([^"]+)">(.*?)</h\1>', body, re.S)
def plain(s):
    s = re.sub(r"<[^>]+>", "", s)
    return html.unescape(s).strip()

nav, open_group = [], False
for lvl, hid, raw in heads:
    t = plain(raw)
    if lvl == "1":
        if open_group: nav.append("</ul></details>")
        label = t.split("—")[0].strip()
        nav.append(f'<details class="navgrp" open><summary>{html.escape(label)}</summary><ul>')
        open_group = True
    elif lvl == "2" and open_group:
        nav.append(f'<li class="l2"><a href="#{hid}">{html.escape(t)}</a></li>')
    elif lvl == "3" and open_group:
        nav.append(f'<li class="l3"><a href="#{hid}">{html.escape(t)}</a></li>')
if open_group: nav.append("</ul></details>")
NAV = "\n".join(nav)

# ---- status chips on the sample headings --------------------------------
CHIP = {
    "simpleP2P": ("waive", "waived · 1 GPU"),
    "greenContext": ("flag", "needs --split"),
    "reductionMultiBlockCG": ("flag", "needs include dirs"),
    "multiGPUGradientAverage": ("flag", "needs mpirun"),
    "Utilities": ("lib", "library"),
}
SAMPLES = set(re.findall(r'<h3 id="([a-z0-9-]+)">([A-Za-z]\w+)</h3>', body))
def chip_for(name):
    k, label = CHIP.get(name, ("pass", "verified"))
    return f'<span class="chip {k}">{label}</span>'
def add_chip(m):
    hid, name = m.group(1), m.group(2)
    return f'<h3 id="{hid}">{name}{chip_for(name)}</h3>'
body = re.sub(r'<h3 id="([a-z0-9-]+)">(\w+)</h3>', add_chip, body)

CSS = """
:root{
  --paper:#F6F7F4; --surface:#FFFFFF; --sunk:#F0F2ED;
  --ink:#171A18; --muted:#5B635D; --rule:#DCE0DA; --rule-soft:#E9ECE6;

JS = """
// scroll-spy: highlight the nav entry for the section currently in view
const links = [...document.querySelectorAll('nav a')];
const byId = new Map(links.map(a => [a.getAttribute('href').slice(1), a]));
const targets = [...document.querySelectorAll('main h1[id], main h2[id], main h3[id]')]
  .filter(h => byId.has(h.id));
let active = null;
const spy = new IntersectionObserver(entries => {
  for (const e of entries) if (e.isIntersecting) {
    const a = byId.get(e.target.id);
    if (!a || a === active) continue;
    if (active) active.classList.remove('on');
    a.classList.add('on');
    active = a;
    const grp = a.closest('details.navgrp');
    if (grp && !grp.open) grp.open = true;
    const nav = document.querySelector('nav');
    if (window.innerWidth > 1000) {
      const r = a.getBoundingClientRect(), n = nav.getBoundingClientRect();
      if (r.top < n.top + 40 || r.bottom > n.bottom - 40)
        a.scrollIntoView({block:'center', behavior:'auto'});
    }
    break;
  }
}, {rootMargin: '-72px 0px -72% 0px', threshold: 0});
targets.forEach(t => spy.observe(t));
"""

❯ Project Directive: Build symoneural_reinforce from Native CUDA Components

  You are tasked with scaffolding a high-performance custom inference and compute engine repository named symonee/MIT_AI_Training/MIT_AI_Training/symoneural_reinforce/.

  This engine must be built entirely from scratch using direct, bare-metal CUDA Python (cuda.core and cuda.bindi CUDA kernels, and CuPy memory management. It must target the local hardware (NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5070 Ti,Blackwell sm_120, 16 GB VRAM). Do not clone, reference, or wrap external third-party inference codebases; design everything natively using the primitives validated in the local cuda-samples curriculum.

  Repository Blueprint & Modules:
  symoneural/core/:

  Device context management, custom stream schedulers, and zero-copy/pinned memory resource pools using cuda.cor

  symoneural/compute/:

  Native weight matrix multiplication and reduction operators utilizing single-pass multi-block cooperative groule) and custom JIT-compiled template kernels via cuda.core.Program.



  symoneural/scheduler/:

  An asynchronous block-based memory allocator and continuous request queue leveraging cuda.graphs for repeating ate launch overhead.

  symoneural/server/:

  A clean FastAPI backend exposing endpoints that route tensors directly through custom CUDA execution streams.

  Strict Constraints:

"""
Vector Addition using CUDA Core API

This sample demonstrates element-wise vector addition: C = A + B
using cuda.core for runtime compilation and kernel launch.
"""

import sys
from pathlib import Path

# Add parent directory to path to import utilities
sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).parent.parent.parent / "Utilities"))
from cuda_samples_utils import verify_array_result  # noqa: E402

try:
    import cupy as cp
    from cuda.core import Device, LaunchConfig, Program, ProgramOptions, launch
except ImportError as e:
    print(f"Error: Required package not found: {e}")
    print("Please install from requirements.txt:")
    print("  pip install -r requirements.txt")
    sys.exit(1)


# CUDA kernel source code
VECTOR_ADD_KERNEL = """
/**
 * CUDA Kernel for vector addition
 * Computes the vector addition of A and B into C.
 */
template<typename T>
__global__ void vectorAdd(const T *A, const T *B, T *C, int numElements)
{
    int i = blockDim.x * blockIdx.x + threadIdx.x;

    if (i < numElements)
    {
        C[i] = A[i] + B[i];
    }
}
"""


def vector_add_cuda_core(num_elements=50000, device_id=0, verify=True):
    """
    Perform vector addition using cuda.core API.

    Parameters
    ----------
    num_elements : int
        Number of elements in each vector
    device_id : int
        CUDA device ID to use
    verify : bool
        Whether to verify the result

    Returns
    -------
    bool
        True if successful, False otherwise
    """
    try:
        # Initialize device
        # Initialize device
        print("[Vector addition using CUDA Core API]")
        device = Device(device_id)
        device.set_current()

        print(f"Device: {device.name}")
        print(f"Compute Capability: sm_{device.arch}")

        stream = device.create_stream()

        # Compile kernel
        print("Compiling kernel 'vectorAdd<float>'...")
        program_options = ProgramOptions(std="c++17", arch=f"sm_{device.arch}")
        program = Program(VECTOR_ADD_KERNEL, code_type="c++", options=program_options)
        module = program.compile("cubin", name_expressions=("vectorAdd<float>",))
        kernel = module.get_kernel("vectorAdd<float>")
        print("Kernel compiled successfully")

        # Allocate and initialize vectors
        print(f"[Vector addition of {num_elements} elements]")
        dtype = cp.float32

        a = cp.random.rand(num_elements).astype(dtype)
        b = cp.random.rand(num_elements).astype(dtype)
        c = cp.empty(num_elements, dtype=dtype)

        # Synchronize before kernel launch
        device.sync()

        # Configure and launch kernel
        threads_per_block = 256
        blocks_per_grid = (num_elements + threads_per_block - 1) // threads_per_block

        print(
            f"CUDA kernel launch with {blocks_per_grid} blocks "
            f"of {threads_per_block} threads"
        )

        config = LaunchConfig(grid=blocks_per_grid, block=threads_per_block)

        # Launch kernel
        launch(
            stream,
            config,
            kernel,
            a.data.ptr,
            b.data.ptr,
            c.data.ptr,
            cp.int32(num_elements),
        )
        stream.sync()

Wrote 50 lines to /home/google/MIT_AI_Training/MIT_AI_Training/symoneural_reinforce/symoneural/core/errors.py
"""Engine exception hierarchy.

The error-handling strategy mirrors what the cuda-samples curriculum verified on
this box (see ``frontend/site/docs/02-setup-environments.md``): a capability that the hardware
genuinely cannot provide is *waived*, not failed. ``simpleP2P`` exits cleanly on a
single-GPU machine and that is the sample behaving correctly; the engine follows
the same convention so that "cannot run here" never masquerades as "broken".
"""

from __future__ import annotations


class SymoneuralError(Exception):
    """Base class for every engine-raised error."""


class DeviceError(SymoneuralError):
    """No usable CUDA device, or the device rejected an operation."""


class CapabilityWaived(SymoneuralError):
    """The hardware cannot provide a capability the caller asked for.

    Distinct from :class:`DeviceError`: nothing is wrong, the box simply does not
    have the feature. Callers are expected to catch this and degrade, the way
    ``simpleP2P`` waives rather than failing when a second GPU is absent.
    """


class CompilationError(SymoneuralError):
    """NVRTC rejected a kernel.

    Carries the kernel name and the compiler log, because the failure mode seen
    most often on this box -- ``cannot open source file "cuda/std/type_traits"``
    from ``reductionMultiBlockCG`` -- is only diagnosable from the log.
    """

    def __init__(self, name: str, log: str, source: str | None = None) -> None:
        self.name = name
        self.log = log
        self.source = source
        super().__init__(f"failed to compile kernel {name!r}:\n{log}")


class AllocationError(SymoneuralError):
    """The allocator could not satisfy a request."""


class SchedulerError(SymoneuralError):
    """Invalid scheduler or graph state transition."""

● Write(/home/google/MIT_AI_Training/MIT_AI_Training/symoneural_reinforce/symoneural/core/context.py)
Wrote 196 lines to /home/google/MIT_AI_Training/MIT_AI_Training/symoneural_reinforce/symoneural/core/context
"""Device context management.

Wraps ``cuda.core.Device`` with the capability facts the engine needs to make
launch decisions, and caches them so hot paths never re-query the driver.

Hardware notes for the target box (measured, see ``frontend/site/docs/01-hardware-dossier.md``):

* RTX 5070 Ti, Blackwell, ``sm_120``, 70 SMs x 128 cores, 15.9 GiB.
* 48 MB L2 -- large enough that any working set under ~100 MB is measuring cache,
  not DRAM. :attr:`DeviceContext.l2_bytes` exists so benchmarks can size past it.
* DRAM ~752 GB/s measured; host<->device capped at ~7 GB/s by a PCIe Gen 3 x8
  host link. :attr:`DeviceContext.pcie_bound` is the flag the scheduler reads to
  decide that stream-overlap is not worth attempting.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any

from .errors import CapabilityWaived, DeviceError

try:
    from cuda.core import Device, system
except ImportError as exc:  # pragma: no cover - import guard
    raise ImportError(
        "cuda-core is required. Install with: pip install 'cuda-core>=1.0.0'"
    ) from exc


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class DeviceCapabilities:
    """Immutable snapshot of everything the engine branches on."""

    device_id: int
    name: str
    arch: str
    compute_capability: tuple[int, int]
    total_memory: int
    multiprocessor_count: int
    max_threads_per_block: int
    max_threads_per_sm: int
    warp_size: int
    l2_bytes: int
    shared_memory_per_block: int
    supports_cooperative: bool
    supports_managed: bool

    @property
    def sm_arch(self) -> str:
        return f"sm_{self.arch}"

    def summary(self) -> str:
        maj, minor = self.compute_capability
        return (
            f"{self.name} | {self.sm_arch} (cc {maj}.{minor}) | "
            f"{self.multiprocessor_count} SMs | "
            f"{self.total_memory / 2**30:.2f} GiB | "
            f"L2 {self.l2_bytes / 2**20:.0f} MB"
        )


def _attr(props: Any, *names: str, default: Any = 0) -> Any:
    """Read the first attribute that exists.

    ``cuda.core`` property names have moved between releases; rather than pin to
    one spelling we probe the alternatives and fall back to a default.
    """
    for n in names:
        try:
            v = getattr(props, n)

            continue
        if v is not None:
            return v
    return default


class DeviceContext:
    """Owns one CUDA device and its capability snapshot.

    Use as a context manager so the device is made current for the duration::

        with DeviceContext(0) as ctx:
            print(ctx.caps.summary())
    """

    def __init__(self, device_id: int = 0) -> None:
        try:
            devices = Device.get_all_devices()
        except Exception as exc:  # pragma: no cover - no driver present
            raise DeviceError(f"could not enumerate CUDA devices: {exc}") from exc

        if not devices:
            raise DeviceError("no CUDA-capable device found")
        if device_id >= len(devices):
            raise DeviceError(
                f"device {device_id} requested but only {len(devices)} present"
            )

        self.device_id = device_id
        self.device = Device(device_id)
        self.device.set_current()
        self._caps: DeviceCapabilities | None = None
        self._device_count = len(devices)

    # -- capabilities ------------------------------------------------------
    @property
    def caps(self) -> DeviceCapabilities:
        if self._caps is None:
            self._caps = self._probe()
        return self._caps

    def _probe(self) -> DeviceCapabilities:
        d = self.device
        p = d.properties
        major = _attr(p, "compute_capability_major", default=0)
        minor = _attr(p, "compute_capability_minor", default=0)
        return DeviceCapabilities(
            device_id=self.device_id,
            name=d.name,
            arch=str(d.arch),
            compute_capability=(int(major), int(minor)),
            total_memory=int(_attr(p, "total_global_mem", "total_memory")),
            multiprocessor_count=int(_attr(p, "multiprocessor_count")),
            max_threads_per_block=int(_attr(p, "max_threads_per_block", default=1024)),
            max_threads_per_sm=int(
                _attr(p, "max_threads_per_multiprocessor", default=1536)
            ),
            warp_size=int(_attr(p, "warp_size", default=32)),
            l2_bytes=int(_attr(p, "l2_cache_size", default=0)),
            shared_memory_per_block=int(
                _attr(p, "max_shared_memory_per_block", "shared_mem_per_block",
                      default=49152)
            ),
            supports_cooperative=bool(
                _attr(p, "cooperative_launch", default=False)
            ),
            supports_managed=bool(_attr(p, "managed_memory", default=False)),
        )

    @property
    def device_count(self) -> int:
        return self._device_count

    @property
    def pcie_bound(self) -> bool:
        """True when host<->device transfer is the limiting factor.

        On the target box the host caps PCIe at Gen 3 x8 (~7 GB/s measured)
        against ~752 GB/s of DRAM bandwidth -- a ~107x gap. The scheduler reads
        this to skip multi-stream overlap, which measured 1.00x / 0.98x / 0.93x
        for 2 / 4 / 8 streams and is therefore pure overhead here.
        """
        return True

    # -- streams -----------------------------------------------------------
    def create_stream(self):
        return self.device.create_stream()

    def sync(self) -> None:
        self.device.sync()

    # -- guards ------------------------------------------------------------
    def require_cooperative(self) -> None:
        """Waive rather than fail when cooperative launch is unavailable."""
        if not self.caps.supports_cooperative:
            raise CapabilityWaived(
                f"{self.caps.name} does not support cooperative kernel launch; "
                "the single-pass multi-block reduction requires it"
            )

    def require_devices(self, n: int) -> None:
        """Waive when there are not enough GPUs, the way simpleP2P does."""
        if self._device_count < n:
            raise CapabilityWaived(
                f"{n} GPUs required, {self._device_count} present -- waiving"
            )

    @staticmethod
    def driver_version() -> str:
        try:
            return str(system.driver_version)
        except Exception:  # pragma: no cover
            return "unknown"

    # -- context manager ---------------------------------------------------
    def __enter__(self) -> DeviceContext:
        self.device.set_current()
        return self

    def __exit__(self, *exc: object) -> None:
        self.sync()

    def __repr__(self) -> str:
        return f"<DeviceContext {self.caps.summary()}>"
